November 21 · The [November 18 case-study manuscript](../../reports/descent-trace-repair/) is consolidated as the paper's first milestone, with a [claim-to-evidence memo](../../reports/descent-trace-repair/milestone.md) and the standalone release bundle built and checksum-verified against `FILES.txt` (117 files, revision `7640dc02`); Marcos set November 21 as the paper's external release date. **The evening of November 19 the tree's bulk left this disk with no record** (tracked records restored from git November 21; what the bucket holds, what is lost and how it recomputes: [the loss account](../../records/maintenance-runs/2026-11-19-evening-studies-deletion/README.md)); the verifier's 3,574 drifts traced to that deletion. The bundle of that day used the committed 116-entry list, which leaves out the transfer panel's evidence.

November 22 · What the gate needs is back on this disk from the bucket and the November 18 publication archive, each file held to a recorded hash ([what came back and how](../../records/maintenance-runs/2026-11-22-tree-and-panel-recovery/README.md)); the paper's eight panel records are among them, recovered without grading again. 63 files are in no store; they are named one by one in a [list](../../records/maintenance-runs/2026-11-19-evening-studies-deletion/recorded-losses.json) that `round-2-method.json` binds by hash (`recorded_losses`), each absence excused only to the pre-deletion declarations and archive records that name it, and no native run may read one. The verifier reports no drift. No run was started.

November 19 · The [paired evaluation for the streamed recovery](runs/joint-stock-streamed-threshold-evaluation/declaration.json) selects the sealed checkpoint of the third streamed run at the original 95% endpoint. It uses the same 33 questions, stock comparison and blind arithmetic rubric as the interrupted run's evaluation. The checkpoint reader is the revision bound by the streamed trainer.

November 19 · [Fifth streamed start of the joint training loop](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-streamed-recovery-5/declaration.json) from the saved revision 3, with the same objective, matcher, step fraction, threshold and solver. Four trainer sources differ from the fourth start's: an install writes only the pages of the model file whose bytes differ and the elements of the working weights whose bits differ, and the direction and the displacement are in RAM again, so the working files are the model, the working weights and the BF16 rounding remainder (24,329,444,544 bytes, where they were 39,895,039,168). It is an open revision of the fourth streamed start's declaration, bound to the independent review of that change, which found that no computed value changes and asked that the first start with this build be held to the recorded updates. It is stopped at the first difference from the continuation's recorded updates 0 to 4; it runs with a 120 MB/s read limit and no write limit, and its unit's memory, reads and writes are sampled beside the free disk. Stated before it starts: the trainer's anonymous memory is expected to reach 24 to 28 GB between the preview and the end of the installed response, where the fourth start held 8 to 13 GB. [Build record](../../records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md#the-fifth-streamed-start) **Ended 6:35 PM CT November 19, killed in the middle of update 8's install on Marcos's stop: no exit checkpoint, updates 5 to 7 measured (0.0038739118, 0.0038746453, 0.0038732241 against the 0.0013593606 threshold), no result claimed; its page-cache refaults slowed the whole machine (`records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md`, "The fifth start was killed").**
November 19 · [Fourth streamed start of the joint training loop](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-streamed-recovery-4/declaration.json) from the saved revision 3, with the same objective, matcher, step fraction, threshold and solver. Two trainer sources differ from the third start's: an array a phase releases stays in the kernel's page cache where the earlier build flushed it and dropped it, and a phase's end waits for its files' background writes, so a write the disk refused stops the run. It is an open revision of the third streamed start's declaration, bound to the independent review of that change, which found that no value a reader sees can differ; it runs with no write limit, on that review's finding, and its unit's reads, writes and the machine's I/O pressure are sampled beside its memory and the free disk. It is stopped at the first difference from the continuation's recorded updates 0 to 4. [Build record](../../records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md#the-fourth-streamed-start) **Stopped on purpose at 1:49 PM CT after update 2**: every record of its baseline and updates 1 and 2 equals the continuation's (34 records compared), and nothing past update 2 was measured. The forced eviction was gone and its unit read about 55 MB/s, but each update still read about 98 GB and wrote about 56 GB, because an install rewrote the whole model file and the whole working-weights file for about 22,000 changed values and the direction and the displacement were files; its exit checkpoint of update 2 is on the disk, and the fifth streamed start above replaces it. [Where a step's disk traffic came from](../../records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md#the-fourth-streamed-start)
November 19 · [Third streamed start of the joint training loop](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-streamed-recovery-3/declaration.json) from the saved revision 3, with the first streamed start's trainer sources byte for byte, its unit as it was, and the same objective, matcher, step fraction, threshold and solver. It starts with more than 130 GB free, where the second start's check found 124.41 GB, and the free disk is sampled beside the memory while it runs. It is an open revision of the second streamed start's declaration, bound to the same independent review, and it is stopped at the first difference from the continuation's recorded updates 0 to 4. [Build record](../../records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md) **Stopped on purpose at 11:49 AM CT after update 4**: every record of its baseline and updates 1 to 4 equals the continuation's (the gate exited 0 at 11:41 AM CT), and nothing past update 4 was measured. Its trainer build flushed and dropped every released page, so its unit had read 2.38 TB and written 258 GB by 11:24 AM CT and the machine lagged; its exit checkpoint of update 4 is in the bucket, and the fourth streamed start above replaces it. [What it did to the disk, and the change](../../records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md#what-the-third-start-did-to-the-disk-and-the-change)
November 19 · [Second streamed start of the joint training loop](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-streamed-recovery-2/declaration.json) from the saved revision 3, with the first streamed start's trainer sources byte for byte and the same objective, matcher, step fraction, threshold and solver. Its declaration names the run in the consumed-trace archive's list, which the first start's did not, and its unit fixes the allocator's mapping threshold at glibc's initial value so freed arrays go back to the kernel. It is an open revision of the first streamed start's declaration, bound to the same independent review, and it is stopped at the first difference from the continuation's recorded updates 0 to 4. [Build record](../../records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md) **Stopped at 7:13 AM CT while measuring update 1**: its 18 written records matched the continuation's, and the trainer's disk reserve then found 76.05 GB free against the 76.10 GB it asks before an acquisition, after about 22.8 GB went to other use of the disk during the run. Its unit peaked at 14.87 GB. Nothing past update 1 was measured; the third streamed start above replaces it.
November 19 · [Streamed resume of the joint training loop](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-streamed-recovery/declaration.json) from the saved revision 3 with the same objective, matcher, step fraction, threshold and solver. The trainer's leaves, remainders, direction and displacement live in files and are paged in one piece at a time, gradients arrive one leaf at a time, a native trace is read one merge at a time from its capture, and nothing opens the display. It is an open revision of the second start's declaration, bound to the independent review, and it is stopped at the first difference from the continuation's recorded updates 0 to 4. [Build record](../../records/development/2026-11-19-streamed-working-state/README.md) **Stopped at 5:39 AM CT after update 1**: it reproduced the continuation's baseline and update 1 in all 22 compared records, installed update 1, and its own declaration then failed the consumed-trace archive step, which archives only for a run the declaration's list names. Its unit peaked at 15.6 GB against the continuation's 63.4 GB. Nothing past update 1 was measured; the second streamed start above replaces it.
November 19 · [Second start of the disk-backed joint resume](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-disk-recovery-2/declaration.json) from the saved revision 3 with the same objective, threshold and solver. The first start was stopped at its baseline, which matched the interrupted run bit for bit, after its trainer was measured at 34.7 GB of anonymous memory before any gradient. This start reads dense remainders straight into the working file and returns freed pages after each phase.
November 18 · [Disk-backed restart of the joint training loop](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-disk-recovery/declaration.json) uses the saved revision 3, original stock loss threshold and same solver. Its predecessor exited at 10:50 p.m. CT after four additional installs, without a terminal checkpoint.

November 18 · [Joint stock loss continuation](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-loss-continuation/declaration.json) resumes revision 3 of the same joint training lineage. The endpoint is measured group loss at or below 5% of its original stock value.

November 18 · The [fixed-history FFN20 native readback](runs/early-27-ffn20-native-effect/README.md) measured a projection change of −0.05413812 toward the correct-witness target. Subtraction remained the greedy choice on the fixed original history.

November 18 · [Joint stock training target](runs/joint-stock-target/declaration.json) packages the operation-specific downhill comparison, the conditional climb field and valid friction into one shared weight update. The active repair cases are 91 J and 95 J.

November 18 · The [pristine stock origin for the joint fit](runs/joint-pristine-stock-origin/README.md) verified the published BF16 model, measured the known 91 J descent failure on GPU0 and typed 538 native tensors with an empty optimizer remainder. November 18 · [Stock fitted climb reading](runs/joint-stock-climb-fit-reading/declaration.json) evaluates the newly frozen climb field on the historical 95 J climb failure using the complete native free generation. November 18 · [Conditional climb overlap](runs/joint-climb-conditional-overlap/declaration.json) asks whether the full trace distinguishes the extra-height error when every donor makes the same downhill-sign mistake. November 18 · [Crossed histories on two physically successful descent repairs](runs/same-input-crossed-success-histories/README.md) found both repaired weights still above the historical bound on early history, and early weights already within it on repaired history. November 18 · [Native same-input replay of the failed projected edit](runs/same-input-failed-repair/README.md) measured signed squared reference-distance changes of +1.94 × 10⁻⁷ at descent and +7.82 × 10⁻⁹ at friction. On its historical signed descent direction, the [two-weight, two-history replay](runs/same-input-failed-repair/readout/history-control/result.json) attributed 88.90–94.71% of diagonal movement to changing the supplied generation history, depending on intervention order.

November 18 · [Same-input comparison across three physical edits](runs/same-input-operation-comparison/README.md) found that the two repairs moved the full trace more similarly than either did to the failed edit at all 73 predictions before their histories split on the fitted case. November 18 · [Finite installed diagnostic replay](runs/finite-installed-diagnostic-replay/README.md) reproduced the historical positive finite response, and the automatically registered earlier score moved +0.00003330 in the finite graph versus +0.00004284 natively while remaining inside tolerance.

November 18 · [Block 0 recorded-QKV mixer readout](runs/directional-intervention-block0-recorded-qkv-mixer/README.md) recovered the native mixed-output motion on single-column decode calls. Most of the multi-column prefill discrepancy remained.

November 18 · [descent-operand-identity](runs/descent-operand-identity/declaration.json) completed on 30 public original responses and five saved controls. November 18 · [descent-multiple-references-retrospective](runs/descent-multiple-references-retrospective/declaration.json) compares two multiple-reference readings of the previously located development cell using saved turns and energy-group exclusion. Read this first. It is the status page, the method and the vocabulary for round two.

## Current direction — November 16: one pair, differentiate computation into weights, regenerate

November 17 completed single-update comparison, `vector-chance-isolated-pre-error-update` and `vector-chance-isolated-pre-error-device-continuation`. The directional arm lowered fitted loss by 0.290% while its targeted excess rose 2.91%. All three directional responses reproduced the original answer exactly. Standard raised fitted loss 5.55-fold. The two native installations differed at 2,056 values across 84 tensors. The independent question retained the wrong descent and verdict. [Nine measured outcomes](../../records/development/2026-11-17-isolated-pre-error-device-continuation/RESULTS.md).

November 17 pre-sign context diagnostic, `vector-chance-descent-context-175`: both natural replay controls reproduced their complete histories and full sign logits. Swapping the pre-sign framing at frozen 175 changed the fitted case from 91 J to 11 J and the unfitted case from 55 J to the correct 115 J, preserving each original question and its operands. In `vector-chance-descent-context-heading-175`, changing only heading choice 72 induced the fitted sign error, while the unfitted case still added a negative potential-energy change and produced 55 J. [Single-choice result](../../records/development/2026-11-17-descent-context/heading.md). [Experiment](../../records/development/2026-11-17-descent-context/README.md).

November 17 preservation diagnostic, `vector-chance-reference-preservation-348`: same-device correct-prompt trace loss rose from 0.0000001300274 to 0.00009352030, with all 105 merge contributions higher. Both generated solutions correctly calculate 44 J available, 20 J required and YES. [Native comparison](../../records/development/2026-11-17-reference-preservation/reference-preservation-result.md) · [Arithmetic and first changed token](../../records/retrospectives/2026-11-17-one-pair-correction/answer-audit.md).

November 17 unfitted development diagnostic, `vector-chance-development-transfer-348`: on the [prospectively selected prompt](../../records/retrospectives/2026-11-17-one-pair-correction/development-case.md) trace loss rose 62.72%. Frozen 348 corrected required energy from 60 to 30 J and the verdict from NO to YES. Both models subtracted the descent and gave 55 J available instead of 115 J. [Native evidence](../../records/development/2026-11-17-reference-preservation/development-transfer-result.md).

November 17 crossed histories, `vector-chance-crossed-history-175-189`: four checkpoint-by-history captures plus two same-device free controls reproduce the early 56.26% decrease. Supplying the later history at fixed weights accounts for 99.66–99.80% of the diagonal decrease, depending on intervention order. Both models freely generated their own observed route. The [native prefix analysis](../../records/development/2026-11-17-crossed-history/README.md) locates the first different consumed input at 554. This is a 14-update interval.

November 17, `vector-chance-family-swap-175-189`: the two complementary native swaps reproduce the generated history of the donor of the feed-forward down-projection weights on both attention-output backgrounds. At both endpoints all energy calculations were already correct before the paths diverged in the final restatement. The four complete generations of `vector-chance-development-profile-175-189` reproduce the same correct reference arithmetic and the same partial unfitted-case repair at both early checkpoints. At 348 the development trace score is higher while the underlying descent error remains the same.

November 17, [`vector-chance-descent-sign-swap`](runs/vector-chance-descent-sign-swap/result.json): the early control subtracts the 40 J descent gain and gives 11 J available and NO. Transplanting either the later attention-output family alone or the later feed-forward down-projection family alone produces the correct addition, 91 J available and YES on the same GPU and prompt. The attention-only hybrid reproduces the late generated history; the feed-forward-only hybrid solves it with different wording. The early control reproduces the historical arithmetic error on a different history.

**One-pair recovery stopped November 18 at 00:26 CT.** The native checkpoint at cumulative 390 retains weights and remainders. Fresh fitted loss was 0.00006405936 and the generated answer gave 91 J, 20 J and YES. [Exit receipt](runs/vector-chance-final-experiment-installed-348-recovery/final-state.json) · [Training record](../../final-experiment/training-and-stopping.md). **November 17 full-weight training.** Storage interrupted training at 15:48 CT after installation of historical step 348 and before its fresh observation. The independently reviewed learner launched at 18:45 CT in `vector-chance-final-experiment-installed-348-recovery`. Its working-state receipt confirms exact restoration of native values and FP32 remainders. Fresh loss fell from 0.00006429224470 at 347 to 0.00006420801373 at 348 to 0.00006416053124 at 349 and 0.00006411269944 at 350, with unchanged history and correspondence. The [phase assessment](../../records/retrospectives/2026-11-17-full-weight-phase-review/ASSESSMENT.md) follows the Final Experiment and its one-pair scope. [Recovery review](../../records/retrospectives/2026-11-17-unmeasured-state-recovery-review.md) · [Declaration](runs/vector-chance-final-experiment-installed-348-recovery/declaration.json).

**Prospective single-transition response measurement — November 16:** `vector-chance-precision-response-continuation` will retain the exact last completed state of `vector-chance-ffn-precision-continuation`, measure one prescribed update through its continuous prediction, actual BF16-displacement JVP, nonlinear graph with anchors retained at the starting weights, native held replay with unchanged default callback geometry, and ordinary fresh native path; subsequent iterations keep the same loop. The step rule, empirical eta, original reference/tolerance and complete105merge scope stay fixed. Two lifecycle/partial-path fixtures, ten geometry fixtures with six field subcases, actual saved-index format/coverage and Eli source review pass. ## Historical direction — November 12: correction, not protection

Marcos directly corrected the construction: **protection belongs nowhere**. There is one objective, correction of the computation. Where the current trace already agrees with the correct pattern, there is no correction or only the small correction its discrepancy warrants. Where it diverges, correction applies, including size. There is no separate preservation objective, protection region, bound-feasibility goal or bringing-back phase in the new construction. His authorized phone relay needs no repeated approval. The correction and the withdrawal of Astra's contrary recommendation are in `journal/2026-11-12-correction-not-protection.md`.

The prospective saved-state construction is `records/retrospectives/2026-11-12-correction-objective-astra.md`: one common correct-pattern error in direction and log size, at every registered answer place for both groups, with small errors contributing small corrections rather than amplified violations. A native BF16 candidate from the state after controlled-bf16-4's nineteen matrices is compared with that unchanged state on its own whole-model computation, before another full sweep. The solver, frozen reference and bounded A/B comparisons are complete; their outcomes below do not establish a completed full-path repair. `runs/correction-at-19/` declares the original-reference construction and diagnostic-control phase (step 3) and, since November 12, the single-matrix edit phase with its numerical configuration (step 5). Its reference is made (2:32 AM CT, November 12): the model's last merge is read as what output_norm passes on, and the reference first made at 1:59 AM CT with that merge read raw is kept beside it as `reference-raw-terminal`. Status: Fable completed the candidate and checked native model copy at 3:38:11 AM CT, November 12. The fitting folds selected ridge 2^-4, round 40; their held-fold score is 0.000904 versus 0.004250 initially, and the all-fitting given-text objective is 0.004166 to 0.0000209. Those are construction readings, not independent validation or a repair result. The primary own-trace result is complete and meets the declared weakening condition (`runs/correction-at-19/result.json`): own L rises from after-19's 0.21662 to 5.19145; complete fitting answers change from 3/15 wrong and 10/15 correct to 7/15 and 3/15. With after-19's own text held fixed, L falls to 0.20664; on the original histories the sign margin favors plus in all fifteen eligible wrong cases, but these are conditional readings, not fifteen own repairs. The completed capture is assigned to `own/candidate/`; further conditional records and the declared crossed-opening diagnostic belong under `own/diagnostics/`. Supplementary readings do not select another candidate or replace the unforced primary result. The capture relocation is complete (`own/candidate/provenance.json`). ## Status

**Latest direction, November 10:** Marcos rejected changing the model to float32: the problem is then the solver, and float32 is not acceptable. The float32 trial below is a rejected construction whose partial records are retained, not an approved path forward, and nothing is judged against a float32 reading (Marcos, the same evening: float32 is to be treated as if it had never existed). See `journal/2026-11-10-controlled-training.md`. The solver now works in the file's own BF16: a step is written as a whole instead of weight by weight, and which matrix is kept is decided among BF16 matrices that hold every bound (both under "Where every number comes from"). On stored rows of the original model, one matrix at a time with nothing changed upstream, the fitting answers at layer 3's attention are left 0.493 of their tube loss inside every bound, where the solver before left 0.985; the answers no fit used are left 0.535 with one size cell of 960 at 1.55 of its bound (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/README.md`). That is development evidence on four matrices, not a training.

The A rung exists (`a2-path`, November 10). Built under the one construction every rung uses and stopped at 18 of its 70 matrices (layers 0 to 8), the edited model, answering on its own, adds the descent in 29 of the 30 answers that subtracted it and has 17 of them complete; with no protection it breaks 12 of the 30 correct answers, and outside the fit it completes 7 of round one's 21 failures and breaks 4 of its 7 correct answers. Its own traces moved 0.79 of the way along the gap over places 142 to 313 and about one gap to the side, in both groups. The B rung (`b2-path`, November 10) is the same 18 matrices and construction built again from the original, with the 12 broken correct answers added as rows that keep their state. It failed its prediction: 4 of the 12 held answers are complete, 13 of the 18 that were not held broke, and 4 of the 30 wrong answers are complete, although all 30 now add the descent. Rows held on the given text did not hold the model's own answers, which leave that text at the fourth token. The C rung (`c2-path`, November 10) corrects a2-path's 18 matrices, with the rows of the answers a2-path damaged asked for the original state and the rows of the answers it repaired asked for a2-path's. At the opening the two sets of rows are the same inputs, the fit served neither ask, and its model completes 12 of the 30 wrong answers and 16 of the 30 correct ones; the reading with the damaged answers alone (`c2-protected`) completes 11 and 14. The D rung (`d2-path`, November 10) is a2-path's construction built again from the original, with the size of each change counted by what it does to the inputs of eight ordinary requests. Its traces are the closest of the round to what the edit aims at: over places 142 to 313 the wrong answers lie 1.05 of the way to the correct pattern with 0.78 gaps to the side, and the correct answers travelled 0.73 gaps (a2-path 0.79, 1.04 and 1.27). The available energy is right in 23 of the 30 wrong answers and 23 of the 30 correct ones (a2-path 17 and 18), and a2-path's shift at the fourth token is gone. But 7 of each 30 are complete: 31 of the 60 answers state the required energy as climb plus descent, 16 of them answers that were correct; the later exploratory comparison at places 312 to 335 found different projections along the gap, not a return to the wrong pattern: the compared answer subsets remained two gaps or more to the side. On unseen ordinary text its states moved 0.60 to 0.74 as far as a2-path's, not under half. No rung of this round shows what D2 has to show. All four rungs are run. Next is the assessment with Marcos: he said on November 10 to go on to C2 and D2, then assess everything and redo the round, and that this round is information for the next one rather than the paper's result. Not yet scored for any rung: the 67 capability questions, the 40 seen held-out questions and round one's 8 extra; round one's controls and ordinary requests are scored for a2-path and d2-path only. The records of c2-path, c2-protected and d2-path were committed after their runs: each declaration was written before its run and could not be committed then, because the verifier's pre-commit check was refusing every commit over a false copy detection (corrected on Marcos's word on November 10). The check that a declaration is committed before its result was removed the same day on his word (`journal/2026-11-10-controlled-training.md`, commit `146e13f2`), and the three are recorded as they stand. d2-path's declaration carries one dated revision, added at 6:43 AM CT when its build had written 3 of its 18 matrices: it withdraws and tightens reasoning and wording after Astra's review and leaves the rule, the parameters and the prediction as declared. The declaration as it stood before that revision is not kept as a separate file; the revision quotes what it withdrew. The construction: every position of the wrong answers' own computation is a row of the fit, asked to move where the patterns diverge and to stay where it was elsewhere; each matrix is aimed at the state right after its merge (`state-overlap`); the fit is on the model's own expression for that state, its strength chosen on the edit as applied; the matrices are solved in path order, each on what arrives with the earlier edits in the model. An edit is judged only by the edited model's own answers and their traces (Marcos, November 9 and 10): readings on given text during a build are not a test and did not predict the answers. The four earlier A2 edits (`a2`, `a2-pattern`, `a2-sent`, `a2-diverge`) are records of wrong conversions, not the A rung. The redo is being prepared as one controlled training (steps 5 and 6 below, rewritten on November 10 from Marcos's words of that day). Its objective and acceptance are Astra's (`records/retrospectives/2026-11-10-stable-training-review-astra.md`), its solver and sweep this seat's (`code/signal-trace/pipeline/weight_edit/tube.py`); so far they are read only on stored rows of the original model, with no model run (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/`). Two things measured there change how it is built. The model file keeps a weight to about one part in 256, so most of a small change was never written and the rest went in as whole steps: the same solve at layer 3's attention leaves 0.985 of the divergence in the file's format and 0.636 with the matrices kept as float32, the same values in a format that can hold a small change. And the engine does not multiply the same way when it reads several tokens at once as when it generates one token at a time, so a pass that reads an answer as given text is not the computation the model answers in; every pass of the training now feeds the answer's tokens one at a time, as the model produced them. Its first run, `controlled-sweep-1`, was stopped at 6:51 PM CT on November 10 after Marcos rejected converting the model to float32. The copy was created and baseline capture attempted, but no weight fitting or completed baseline/reference was retained; `runs/controlled-sweep-1/result.json` records the stop, not a trained-model result. The next construction must remain BF16.

On November 9 this page was cleaned at Marcos's request. An agent had written a plan here that narrowed the method to single tokens, matched pairs, a patch test and one matrix. That plan and its vocabulary are removed. The runs that followed it are listed under "Not the method".

## The hypothesis
The hypothesis targets reasoning and capability, with the belief that closing the gap of the shared capability traces between correct and wrong answers that strongly use that capability not only fixes it but also generalizes.

When a model uses a capability, the signal passing through its weights leaves a trace. Answers that strongly use the same capability share that trace, and where correct and wrong answers part the shared trace shows a gap. Closing the gap by changing the weights should fix the wrong answers. Because the gap belongs to the capability and not to one question, the fix should carry over to questions it was never built from.

There is evidence that the fix is possible (round one and the deleted round-two edits) and evidence that it carries over (the held-out questions). Round two builds the edit from the overlap, which no surviving run has done yet.

Round two succeeds if the trace of the wrong answers is brought into the correct pattern where the two diverged, the rest of the trace stays where it was, and both hold on answers and questions the training never used. What the edited model writes is reported beside that and is not the judge (Marcos, November 10). His goal for it is unchanged: every wrong answer corrected, no correct answer broken, and the repair carries over. The learnings then go into round three, which is about making the technique a loop that runs itself, and the paper is finished.

## What the method means
These journal pages record Marcos's clarifications of November 7. They are guidance, and where this page and they disagree, they hold.
- `journal/2026-11-07-clarifications.md`: what the trace is and why a partial trace is enough.
- `journal/2026-11-07-multitrace-purpose.md`: what the overlap is and how failures are grouped.
- `journal/2026-11-07-round-two-restart.md`: what went wrong in the first attempt.
- `journal/2026-11-10-controlled-training.md`: his direction of November 10, after the four rungs were run. Protection is part of the training, an edit is judged by the trace and not by the answers the model writes, and no number is chosen. They replace steps 5 and 6 as they stood.

In short:
- The trace is the whole path: every token of the answer and every merge, with what came in, what each transformation added and what went on.
- The overlap keeps that progression. It is not an average activation, a read vector, one subtraction of two answers, or a set of large weights.
- A partial recurring influence is enough. It does not have to be exclusive to physics or complete.
- Where to edit, how many matrices and the form of the edit are outcomes of the comparison. They are not chosen before the discrepancy is seen and not inherited from round one.
- The first attempt failed in its implementation, by "restricting observation, averaging passages, selecting one layer's projection, and evaluating outcomes without the full before/after group trace comparison". It was not a failure of the hypothesis.

## Vocabulary
- **Capability.** Here, energy accounting on the slope questions: an object starts moving, descends, loses energy on a rough patch, then climbs.
- **Trace.** For every generated token of an answer, how the signal turns at each of the 105 merges (35 layers, three additions each), together with the inputs and outputs of the matrices that produced each turn.
- **Correct group.** The answers with both energy fields and the verdict right.
- **Failure group.** The wrong answers that share their first incorrect calculation. A correct verdict does not erase an earlier wrong calculation, and the kinds stay separate. Three kinds are known: the climb measured from the start, so the descent is added to it; the descent subtracted from the energy the object has; the descent left out.
- **Overlap.** The traces of a group, aligned passage by passage and compared with their progression kept: what recurs across the members.
- **Discrepancy, or gap.** Where the overlap of a failure group departs from the overlap of the correct group, and recurs across members.
- **Spillover.** How far an edit moves the model on unrelated text.
- **A2, B2, C2, D2.** The four rungs of the ladder, below.

## The method
1. **Overlap the correct answers**: their full traces, every token and every merge.
2. **Overlap the wrong answers** the same way, each failure group separately.
3. **Take the discrepancy** between the overlaps along the whole path, keeping what recurs across members. Where it appears is reported, not chosen in advance.
4. There is no step here. Nothing stands between the discrepancy and the edit. The edited model's complete own computational path tests the discrepancy; proving a perfect target first is not an additional experiment gate.
5. **Train the edit on the whole computation discrepancy.** One objective includes direction and size for both groups on their current own histories. Where computation agrees it asks no or minimal correction; where it diverges it supplies correction. All represented path rows and 105 merges remain, including previously agreeing places after an edit. The original reference and its frame mass stay fixed. No separate protection stage, selected-token target, word-match objective or answer-value verdict measures progress. Acquisition still generates tokens and records their provenance; computational correspondence, not required identical wording, connects histories. Native stored weights remain BF16, settings are source-derived, and every run requires its prospective declaration and verifier. No no-fit answer becomes a fit, calibration or selection input.
6. **Remeasure and correct from the model reached.** After each pass acquire its own whole path, rebuild the remaining requests against the unchanged original reference, and repeat. Measured closure completes correction; a lower scalar, an unchanged pass, a numerical stall or an interruption does not. Inspect answers only after closure, descriptively rather than as a progress or closure score. The exact numerical closure predicate and revised executable loop remain unfinished, and current execution is held. Earlier runs retain their original records, not live admissions.

Until November 10 step 5 was an edit at full strength with no protection, and step 6 a ladder of three further edits on it (B2, C2, D2). Those four rungs were run that day. They are in the status above and under Runs, as information for this training and not as the round's result.

## Historical parameterization through controlled-bf16-4

The following records the protection-based constructions actually run. For the new correction-only construction, use the current direction and linked specification above; these historical bounds are not new-run acceptance rules.
- Where a move is asked: the places and merges where the two original patterns are further apart than two halves of one group are (gap > 2 chance, from the overlap of the states: `state-overlap`, and from November 11 `state-overlap-2`, the same overlap with a matrix's input taken as the engine multiplied it, which is what a sweep declared after that day is aimed at).
- How far: to the nearest point of what two halves of the correct group differ by, a ball of radius sqrt(2 chance) around the correct pattern. Inside it nothing is asked.
- How far anything else may move follows the run's declared preservation policy. Through `controlled-bf16-3`, each group's mean squared protected row movement Q at each registered answer place is limited by V/N, the variance of its original members' registered means over their original count, for direction and log size, with the original-only row floor. **The next new sweep tests the approved mean/row split below:** protected group-mean movement M is limited by V/N, while Q is limited by original empirical row spread T=(N-1)V/N+A, each with its own original-only floor. Earlier declarations and references are unchanged; absence of a split policy is not permission to reinterpret an old run.
- For each question, the existing budget is unchanged: at every place of the prompt the variance of its group's questions over the number of members, and over the question's positions the mean of that (Astra, November 10; until then a question's bound was only how far two readings of it lay apart, which is the engine's arithmetic and allowed nothing). The existing original-only question floors remain. Original groups and counts, currently 30 and 30, supply the reference, whichever answers a fit uses.
- What movement is counted from: the original model, at each of its 105 merges, in every sweep. What earlier sweeps moved remains part of the movement counted; no model's own noise widens what it is allowed. The rejected float32 trial counted format-induced movement too, but conversion is no longer an accepted construction.
- How a pass reads an answer: the question as the model reads it, then the answer's own tokens fed one at a time (single-token teacher replay). Read as given text, several tokens at once, the engine rounds what goes into each matrix to the file's coarse format first: the merge then lies 0.0001 to 0.0007 from what the same weights give one token at a time (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/operator_cpu.out`). Read as generated, the engine reads the question's positions and each answer's first step at once (that step is decided at the prompt's last position) and every later step alone. A solve takes a matrix's input the same way, rounded at the rows read at once and as recorded at the others (`tube.multiplied`, November 11), and counts everything from the engine's own original output: the merge it rebuilds is then the engine's output to 1e-6 at every matrix of the original file (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/expression-check.out`). Until then a solve took every row as recorded, and what it read at the first place and at the questions was not what the engine read.
- What the matrices are kept in: the original BF16 format. Marcos rejected float32 on November 10: the solver must work with native rounding, rather than changing the model's precision (`journal/2026-11-10-controlled-training.md`). The stored-row BF16/float32 comparison is evidence of solver sensitivity, not permission to convert the model. Internal higher-precision solver arithmetic is distinct from the weights that are actually stored and executed; only the BF16-written change is judged.
- How a step is written into the file's BF16 (the solver, November 10): BF16 keeps a weight to about one part in 256, and most of what a step asks of a weight is less than half of that. Written weight by weight, each weight either stays or moves a whole step, and that rounding alone put bounded parts of the trace 1.6 to 19 times past their bounds (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/bf16-writing.jsonl`). A step is therefore written as a whole: the input coordinates are written in the file's order, each weight to its nearest BF16 value, and what a coordinate's rounding left over is carried onto the coordinates not yet written, in the measure the step was solved in (what the bounds see of the matrix's inputs, plus the stage's ridge; along the one output direction the size bounds see most, what they see is added, a declared approximation). Nothing in that is a number chosen: the order is the file's, the measure is the solve's own. Every state of a solve is a matrix the file holds exactly; no finer copy of the weights is kept, and a written matrix is read through the merge like any trial before it is stood on.
- How a BF16 matrix candidate is kept (decided November 10; in `code/signal-trace/pipeline/weight_edit/tube.py` since that evening): every candidate is the actual BF16 matrix read on all three fitting thirds and their pool. Every preservation bound must hold in every one of those readings. A branch stops at its first violation and retains its last feasible checkpoint; another branch does not stop merely because that trial failed. Among feasible common candidates, rank the pool's tube loss first, the worst third's tube loss only on an exact tie, then the size of the actual BF16 change from the feasible starting matrix. The feasible starting matrix remains a candidate; a repair is kept only if pooled tube loss strictly decreases, with restoration treated separately as before. A mean of three feasible endpoints at the same stage remains an optional candidate, with its own coupled BF16 writing and the same checks and ranking; it is never required or automatically preferred. This replaces the earlier whole-stage veto and worst-first ranking of three different matrices. Earlier development records keep the rule they were made with. The thirds are construction/model-selection data, not independent tests.
- When an earlier change left a bound outside at a later matrix: that matrix first brings it back, with every group's bounds and the bounds of all of them together pushing and nothing pulling. If it cannot, the change before it is taken back by halves until it can. Each written merge is also read from what the engine itself puts out at the next read.
- When a sweep is kept (Astra, November 10, "Replay decision after ordering removal" in `records/retrospectives/2026-11-10-stable-training-review-astra.md`): the finished model is read twice, in either order, and each reading has its own verdict. The replay: on the half of the answers no fit used, their own tokens fed one at a time, every bound holds at every one of the 105 merges and they have less left to move than the model the sweep started from (the unedited BF16 model for a first sweep). The model's own answers, which is the test: the answers the original BF16 model and the BF16-edited model write to the same questions, laid on the original axis by the computation alone and read as the whole movement from the original model, are held to the next point. A sweep is kept for the next one when both verdicts hold for the same model, the same frozen reference and the same answers; while one reading is not made, that is unknown, not false. The own answers holding while the replay fails is a finding about the model's own traces with the failed replay beside it: it is not a kept sweep, and it does not show the answers were damaged. The replay holding alone is not a repair. The replay's limits stay as they are for these 15 answers a group: a row's bound is the variance over the original 30, not over 15 and not doubled, and no cell is exempted.
- What a model's own answers are held to (Astra, November 10, after a check on the original recordings, `records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/own-check-rows.out`; the same check of what replaced the row rule is `own-check.out` beside it): the tube on the wrong pattern, and, for each group at each place and merge, how far its pattern moved and how far the spread of its members around the original centre changed, for the state and for its size. Each is held to twice the original answers' variance of that quantity over the number of answers in the mean that is read (15 a group on the answers no fit used), never less than how far a second set of the original file's own answers lies from the first. Rows are held one by one only where the text is the same: in the replay and at the prompt. An answer the model words differently lies as far from the original's, row by row, as another answer of its group does (about 60 times the bound a row is held to in replay, with both group patterns unchanged), so row by row its own answers are only described. This says the recurring pattern of the trace was repaired and kept on these answers; it does not say every single answer is right.
- What only bounds the solver's work (the caps on its iterations, the spacing of the ridges it tries) is listed in each run's declaration with the development record it comes from. None of it decides what counts as inside a bound.
- The numbers of the November 10 ladder (strength one, the answers A2 broke as protected inputs, D2's measured floor) belong to those runs.

### Mean/row split approved November 11, 1:48 PM CT — historical construction

Marcos chose to test the split (the recommended option), as relayed to Astra by dispatch for Eli-93 (`journal/2026-11-11-mean-and-row-preservation.md`). This authorizes the next new sweep to test the proposal at `7b37734e`, not a policy change to a running sweep or a regrading of earlier results. The split is named in that new run's declaration and frozen reference. The implementation and the run's own prediction are recorded before that experiment; this method update is not a claim that it has run.

At each registered answer cell, let a_it be the existing registration weights within member i, summing to one. Let delta_it be the movement already protected by the method: direction or log-size movement counted from the original, with the wrong group's permitted shared direction along the gap removed where a move is asked. For m members in a reading:

    d_i = sum_t a_it delta_it
    M = ||(1/m) sum_i d_i||^2
    Q = (1/m) sum_i sum_t a_it ||delta_it||^2.

Use the corrected original reference rows z_it for the same statistic and registration, and the original group's N members:

    z_i = sum_t a_it z_it
    mu = (1/N) sum_i z_i
    V = (1/(N-1)) sum_i ||z_i-mu||^2
    A = (1/N) sum_i sum_t a_it ||z_it-z_i||^2
    T = (N-1)V/N + A
    M <= max(V/N, F_M)
    Q <= max(T, F_Q).

F_M and F_Q are measured separately as M and Q on the existing original-only floor comparisons. The existing size-expression floor is also measured separately in those two statistics; the baseline rebuilt-versus-engine diagnostic that did not set a floor remains diagnostic. Neither floor is derived from the other, from multiplying a floor-raised old bound, or from an edited model. V and A use the same original-row source for a statistic (including the engine-recorded log sizes where those supply V). The new reference is frozen without overwriting existing references; subsequent accepted sweeps under this policy keep it fixed.

Both bounds replace the old registered-answer bound for direction and log size at all 105 merges, including a layer's last merge. They hold on each of the three existing fitting-third feasibility readings and their pooled reading, using the same original N, V and T, and on the existing unused-answer replay. Recompute the pool's protected movement with its pooled shared-gap component; a squared pooled mean is not an average of squared third means. The reference count does not become five or fifteen when the group read is smaller. This is an explicit original-reference preservation policy, not a sampling-confidence claim about the learned movements.

Nothing changes the differentiated data roles: restoration uses all fitting thirds and their pool without pull; a pull differentiates its solved/held data, with audit and full-pool checks outside that differentiated step. The target, question budgets, own-answer mean/spread budgets and own-start floors, BF16/native checks, candidate selection, and all other acceptance checks remain unchanged. No-fit answers do not become tuning inputs and reserved-40b remains sealed. The row allowance is average displacement up to the original rows' RMS spread, **not** per-answer or distribution/spread preservation. The exact counterexample and the distinction from group-mean protection remain in `records/retrospectives/2026-11-11-mean-and-row-preservation-astra.md`.

## Scoring
- The judge of a training is the trace, as in step 5. The scores below are reported beside it and decide nothing (Marcos, November 10).
- The same scoring as round one, on round one's own questions (the 28 main questions, the 8 extra, the controls and the ordinary requests), so the paper's tables line up.
- Also the 67 capability questions of the existing set and the 40 held-out questions that have already been seen.
- Failures only, per field. Damage to correct answers is reported separately.
- Spillover for every training.
- Reserved-40b stays sealed. It is opened once, after the training is frozen.

## What the training has to show
- The wrong answers' trace is inside the correct pattern's own resolution where the two diverged.
- Everything else in the trace is inside its bounds, for the wrong and for the correct answers.
- Both hold on answers no fit used, and in the edited model's own answers.
- Spillover on other responses is small.
- The same holds on the sealed 40.

## Rules
- **Every run gets its own folder** under `studies/04-round-2-overlap/runs/<run-name>/` and writes nowhere else. The inputs below are read-only.
- **Every run's `declaration.json` is written before any outcome**, with its reasoning, hypothesis, rule and prediction.
- **Bulk data goes to R2, not Git.** Per-answer recordings and arrays are sent with `torad-research traces upload`; Git keeps declarations, summaries and a manifest of what is in the bucket.
- **No borrowed or guessed constants.**
- **This page is updated whenever anything changes.**

- **`recorder-kept-original` (Performance; declared November 15 at 7:22 AM CT, steps 1 and 2: an original-only instrument qualification, no model changed; no engine pass before Astra's admission):** whether the recorder writes the same bytes in less time when the engine's scheduler computes a split as one graph and reads the 212 wanted outputs back after it. `recorder-deferred-original` took the waits at the kept outputs once a split and kept the 212 ranges: the same bytes, 4% faster, so about 4 ms of a kept token's 13.4 is neither the plain decode nor the recorder's records, and by the source each range is keyed and replayed as its own graph on the card (`ggml-cuda.cu`, `ggml_cuda_graph_get_key`); that the ranges are what costs is a hypothesis this run tests, not a finding. On the processor backend 21 of 21 wanted outputs are the installed path's bytes, among them outputs a later node computes into in place (5 ranges where the installed path takes 21). At those rows the unedited feed-forwards 29 to 34 favour the plus, 6 of 6 (-1.57 to -11.72), and so do layers 9 to 28 (-2.11 to -3.95): the plus is written by merges a2-path did not change. But the original's weights reading a2-path's text favour the plus too, at 5 of the 6 rows (-0.92 to -9.87), and would write it; the changed weights add -2.00 to -7.35 on that same text. So the token's flip is carried by the reworded history, and a count of plus signs on own answers is not evidence that the weights changed the decision at the sign (the declaration's own words for this outcome). Read after the predictions and kept apart: a wider row-finder reads 13 of the 15, the original on that text favouring the plus at 12; and a2-path's text first leaves the original's at the answer's fourth token in 19 of 30 answers (' calculation' for ' compact'), a token the original held by 0.23 logits at the median and the weights moved by 0.34. The constructions that edit feed-forward 29 to 34 ask for the plus on the original's own wording, the failing path a2-path walks around; what this means for reading them is in the result. Astra's acceptance assessment (`records/retrospectives/2026-11-13-a2-path-route-assessment-astra.md`) distinguishes wording mediation from conditional weight effects, including the one fixed-history flip; neither route is an acceptance veto, and missing sign rows are not computation verdicts. The existing whole-answer output check is complete on 8 of 15 wrong and 11 of 15 correct answers, not a verified full-trace repair.

- **full-overlap** (steps 1 to 3, run November 9): the full trace of round one's 28 physics questions, every token and all 105 merges; the overlap of the 7 correct answers against the overlap of the 8 answers whose first incorrect calculation subtracts the descent. The 13 other failures are listed with their own first incorrect calculation and kept apart.
  - A left-out answer agrees with its own group's overlap at 0.96 to 0.98 at every depth. The discrepancy keeps its direction when a member is left out (0.95 to 0.96).
  - In 2,914 of 12,600 position-and-merge cells before the first wrong token, every left-out answer falls on its own side. They concentrate at the header of the state after the descent and at the statement naming the descent energy, gained in the correct answers and lost in the failures.
  - Limit: the discrepancy cannot yet be told apart from the question (block against cart, sled, bead, coaster). The answers in other wordings align at too few positions to settle it.
- **a2** (step 5, run November 9): the edit built on full-overlap's discrepancy. All 70 attention-output and feed-forward-down matrices were changed together, at full strength, so that the 8 failing answers' own inputs produce the correct side. Result in `runs/a2/result.json`. Since November 15 the construction (`rows`, `build`, `inputs`) is out of `a2.py`: it took the pre-error positions, which end before the first wrong token. The run's records stay, and `runs/a2/retired-programs.json` names the commit that holds the code.
  - The edited model answered the 28 questions and none of the answers has its fields. It no longer reads the question. No failure repaired, all 7 correct answers broken.
  - On the failing inputs themselves the edit did what was asked in every matrix. The weight change it took was very large: the attention-output matrices changed by a median of 52% of their size, the feed-forward-down matrices by 11%.
  - The build fitted each of 750 inputs to its own target and used the plain difference of the group means everywhere. It did not keep only what recurs across members, as step 3 asks. The next edit carries the recurring part only.
- **wide-overlap** (steps 1 to 3, run November 9): the block question on both sides, 30 answers that subtract the descent and 30 correct ones with the same spread of numbers, recorded with only the turns at all 105 merges for every generated token. No token is marked as the error; the gap is read along the whole trace. Result in `runs/wide-overlap/result.json`, figure in `runs/wide-overlap/where-the-traces-part.png`.
  - Over the first 126 tokens, where every answer has the same structure, the two overlaps run together: a left-out answer falls on its own side 56% of the time.
  - The wording then forks ("Total Energy before rough section" or "Kinetic Energy at bottom"). At the computation that produces that word the groups lean apart, about 0.7 from layer 6 on, without separating.
  - After the fork the gap is large at every depth, layer 0 included, because the groups are then reading different words.
  - Correction: single answers do not separate before the fork, but the gap between the two overlaps does recur there. Computed from two halves of each group that share no answer, it agrees at a cosine of 0.37 (unrelated directions agree at about 0), and at 0.77 after the fork. It is as strong in the first layers as in the deep ones, so part of it comes in with the numbers.
  - So the strong separation before the first wrong token in full-overlap was mostly the object, and in this set the traces part at the fork, where the answer decides how the descent's energy enters.
- **trace-overlap** (steps 1 to 3, run November 9): the 60 traces of wide-overlap, overlapped by the computation alone. Each answer is matched to the others by how alike its turns are, in order; no token, value or answer text lines anything up, and every member is present at every place. Result in `runs/trace-overlap/result.json`, figure in `runs/trace-overlap/where-the-patterns-part.png`.
  - Along one stretch, places 150 to 265 of 480, the gap between the correct pattern and the wrong pattern is 5 to 18 times what chance would give, and two halves that share no answer agree on it at 0.8 to 0.93.
  - Before the stretch the gap is about twice chance and the halves agree at about 0.6. After it the gap fades.
  - The gap is spread over all depths and all three kinds of merge, somewhat larger in layers 14 to 18.
- **a2-pattern** (step 5, run November 9): the edit that brings the wrong pattern's direction towards the correct one, at every place and merge in proportion to the shared part of the gap, as one shared move per place. Result in `runs/a2-pattern/result.json`, figure in `runs/a2-pattern/how-far-the-patterns-moved.png`. Since November 15 the construction (`record`, `build`, `replay`, `moved`, `asked`) is out of `a2_pattern.py`: no token placed it, and it left as a construction on the values of the stream that nothing used. The run's records stay, and `runs/a2-pattern/retired-programs.json` names the commits that hold the code.
  - The matrices changed by a median of 15% (attention) and 8.5% (feed-forward). None of the 88 answers has its fields: no failure repaired, all 30 correct answers of the overlap and all 7 of round one broken. The edited model writes fluent text but garbles the question as it reads it.
  - The same 60 answers replayed token for token through the edited model: the wrong pattern travelled 83.9 and the correct pattern 83.1, where the gap between them was 19.2. The gap went to 26.1. Along the gap the wrong pattern moved 0.50 of the way inside places 150 to 265 and none or backwards after.
  - Why: the conversion from the trace to the matrices. The shared move is 15 to 17% of the turn in the trace; the change asked of the matrix output was 64% of it in attention and 33% in feed-forward, and two thirds of that falls on the tenth of outputs with the smallest learned scale. The raw difference of the merged signal was divided by that scale.
  - The run does not test the hypothesis: the model fails before the calculation.

- **sent-overlap** (steps 1 to 3, run November 9): the same 60 answers and the same alignment as trace-overlap, overlapped where each matrix writes: what each of the 70 matrices sends into the signal, its output with the size taken out. Result in `runs/sent-overlap/result.json`.
  - Inside places 150 to 265 the gap in what the matrices send is about 7 times chance and two halves that share no answer agree on it at 0.88 to 0.89. Before and after the stretch it is about twice chance. Largest in layers 15 to 18.
  - The shared move is about 14% of what a matrix sends inside the stretch, 2% before it and 5% after it.

- **a2-sent** (step 5, run November 9): a2-pattern's edit with one thing changed: each matrix is asked for the shared part of the gap in what it sends, as a part of what it already sends. Result in `runs/a2-sent/result.json`, figure in `runs/a2-sent/how-far-the-patterns-moved.png`. Since November 15 the programs that made it are out of the tree: `a2_sent.py`, and the construction of `a2_pattern.py` it built and read its edit with. The run's records stay, and `runs/a2-sent/retired-programs.json` names the commits that hold the code.

  - Replayed token for token, the wrong pattern moves 1.04 of the way to the correct one inside places 150 to 265, with about 1.1 gaps of sideways travel, and the gap falls to 0.73 of its size. The first matrix alone moves 0.36 of the gap with 0.29 gaps sideways; the earlier edits add up along the path, to 1.5 times the gap in layers 15 to 19. The correct pattern travels as far as the wrong one.

- **a2-diverge** (step 5, run November 9): a2-sent asked only where the two groups diverge, from where the patterns part until they reconcile (Marcos, November 9). A place and matrix counts when the two groups are further apart there than two halves of one group are. Result in `runs/a2-diverge/result.json`. Since November 15 the programs that made it are out of the tree: `a2_sent.py`, and the construction of `a2_pattern.py` it built and read its edit with. The run's records stay, and `runs/a2-diverge/retired-programs.json` names the commits that hold the code.
  - Tested on the edited model's own answers and their traces, not on a replay of the original text.
  - The edited model still leaves the path at about the ninth token and states the final fields without the calculation. Its own traces are alike to the axis at 0.60 where the originals were at 0.86. No failure repaired, all correct answers broken.
  - Where a matrix was asked for nothing, the built edit still changes its output by a quarter to two thirds of what it does where it was asked. So the run does not test where the correction belongs: where a change is asked does not decide where the edit acts.

- **input-overlap** (steps 1 to 3, run November 9 and 10): the same 60 answers, overlapped at what arrives at each matrix: the inputs of the 70 matrices, for both groups. Every answer's own inputs, and the state each matrix adds to, are kept at every generated token and at the question's positions. Result in `runs/input-overlap/result.json`.
  - Where what a matrix sends diverges, the gap between the two groups' inputs is 3.7 times chance and two halves that share no answer agree on it at 0.79. Where the groups follow each other it is 1.4 to 1.5 times chance and the halves agree at 0.4.
  - Inside places 142 to 304 the input gap is 5.6 times chance, at every depth, largest in layers 14 to 23. The places and matrices where the input diverges are the ones where the sending diverges (54% both, 4% only one, 3% only the other).
  - So a weight change can tell where it is from the input, and the correction of one matrix changes what arrives at the next: each matrix is to be fitted on what arrives once the earlier corrections are in the model.
