- **trace-overlap-complete-registered** (steps 1 to 3, completed): recomputed after declaration commit `b3a792a5`, with the retired diagnostic's outcome explicitly known. This is a transparent provenance repair, not a blind prediction or independent replication. Result in `runs/trace-overlap-complete-registered/result.json`.
  - All 30,546 captured native steps from all 60 members and all 105 merges are represented. The 60 pair arrays, two pattern arrays and every recurrence array reproduce the retired diagnostic exactly; details in `reproduction.json`.
  - The longest continuous separation under the existing twice-variation reporting baseline is places 142–313, gap/variation 5.80 and median split-half cosine 0.825. No weight repair is claimed.
  - Solver interface: `members.json`, `pairs/<id>.npy`, `correct-pattern.npy`, `wrong-pattern.npy` and `recurrence.npz`. Keep native input/state rows; average matched place targets per native step. A single reference axis can stretch a correct-member state over multiple places, which remains a recorded limitation.

- **state-overlap** (steps 1 to 3, run November 10): the same 60 answers and the registered correspondence, overlapped where an edit can act: the direction of the state right after each of the 70 matrices' merges. Declared because, in development readings made while fixing `a2-path`'s solver, the shared gaps of the turns turned out not to add up to the gap of the states.
  - The states differ, repeatably, at every matrix: over places 142 to 313 the gap is 4.8 to 9.3 times chance (median 5.7), diverged at 148 to 172 of the 172 places, halves alike 0.83. Outside the region it is about twice chance (1.7 to 3.0), 44% of the cells diverged, halves alike 0.55: the states also differ there, less and less repeatably.
  - At the first merge the turn's gap (0.253) is almost all the gap of the token embeddings (0.254), which no weight produces; the gap of the state after it is 0.043.
  - The shared turn gaps, each delivered in full with the third merges held as they are, would close the state gap through layer 13 and leave more than no edit at 21 of the 70 matrices, from the feed-forward of layer 14 on (last matrix: gap 0.081, they would leave 0.168). As `a2-path` first declared them, with the embeddings inside the first merge, they would leave 0.28 to 0.32 everywhere. This is a statement about the asks, not a prediction of an edited model.
  - What it changes: an edit is aimed at the gap of the states. The overlap of the turns stays the record of where the computation diverges and what an edited model's own traces are read against.

- **a2-path** (step 5, declared November 10, revised the same day before any pass of its build): the A rung under the ladder's one construction. The 30 wrong answers' own computation, every generated step and every question position, is fitted at each of the 70 matrices: a step is asked for the state it had right after that matrix's merge plus the shared part of the gap of that state where the two patterns diverge (`state-overlap`), every other position for the state it had. The change of each matrix is fitted on the model's own expression for that state (the norm, the learned scale and the merge inside), its strength chosen on the edit as applied and rounded, in path order, each matrix on what arrives with the earlier edits in the model, so it is asked for what the ones before it left. A matrix is left as it is when no change does better. No protection of the correct answers. The revision and its reasons are in `runs/a2-path/declaration.json`.

  - Result in `runs/a2-path/result.json`. Stopped after 18 matrices (layers 0–8), before any answer existed, then tested as it stood on its own answers: 17 of the 30 overlap failures complete and 12 of the 30 correct answers broken; outside the fit, 7 of round one's 21 failures complete and 4 of its 7 correct answers broken. Every scored-incomplete answer was read in full (`runs/a2-path/answer-error-review.md`, Astra): before the edit 30 of the 60 answers add the descent, after it 55 do (29 of the 30 that subtracted it; 4 that added it now subtract it). The remaining failures are new errors of other kinds, the same in both groups: the after-climb remainder reported as available, the climb added to the remaining energy, the kinetic energy doubled or halved. The final-triple score is unchanged by that reading; its final-number labels do not always name the written calculation.
  - The model's own traces, laid by the computation alone: over places 142 to 313 the answers that were wrong moved 0.77 (turns) and 0.79 (states) of the way to the correct pattern, every one of the 30, and about one gap to the side; the correct answers travelled 1.26 gaps; the two groups are 0.53 as far apart as before. In full distance the answers that were wrong are 1.15 times as far from the original correct pattern as before. The five answers that subtract the descent after the edit tend to lie less far along the gap (0.30 to 0.62, against 0.49 to 1.26, median 0.79, for the 55 that add it; ordered in 91% of pairs). The movement to the side occurs together with the new errors; that it causes them is not shown.
  - The readings on the given text during the build did not predict this: before the 19th matrix they showed the rows further from the target than before any edit.

- **b2-path** (step 6, declared November 10; overlap answers audited the same day): the B rung. a2-path with one thing changed: the 12 correct answers of the overlap that a2-path broke (the construction's own answers; the four round one answers it broke stay outside the fit) are rows of the fit, every step and question position of their own computation, asked for the state they had. The same 18 matrices (layers 0 to 8), target, conversion, path order and test. The other 18 correct answers and round one's 28 are not fitted and are reported apart. Its prediction and what would weaken it are in `runs/b2-path/declaration.json`.

  - Native-answer scores in `runs/b2-path/answers.json`: 4 of 30 wrong overlap cases complete, 4 of 12 held correct cases complete, and 5 of 18 unheld correct cases complete. Astra read all 47 incomplete overlap answers in full (`runs/b2-path/answer-error-review.md` and `.json`): 46 numerically add the descent to their stated initial energy, but ten also write a contradictory subtraction formula and only 35 reach the correct after-friction number. In the requested Start-of-Descent layout, 30 of 32 incomplete answers reach that correct number, then mishandle the climb, height reference, or energy fields; the other two already miscalculate initial kinetic energy. The 15 incomplete numbered answers include nine initial-energy arithmetic errors and the one numerical descent subtraction. All 18 limit-flagged incomplete answers already contain errors before stopping. Scores are unchanged. Both layouts contain successes and failures; an early layout switch remains a possible contributor, not an isolated cause. This is an answer audit, not a new trace reading or a change to C2.
  - Result in `runs/b2-path/result.json`. The prediction failed on the answers and held on the traces. Held 4 of 12 complete, not held 5 of 18 (a2-path had broken none of those 18), round one 2 of 7 correct and 6 of 21 failures complete. Which correct answers break did not carry from one edit to the next.
  - The calculation the edit aims at was not lost: all 30 answers that subtracted the descent add it in their numbers, and 59 of the 60 do. What fails is the rest of the answer, in text the original never wrote: 18 of 60 stop at the limit, median 686 steps.
  - The model's own traces over places 142 to 313: the answers that were wrong lie 0.63 of the way to the correct pattern (a2-path 0.79) and 1.00 gaps to the side (1.04); the correct answers travelled 1.08 gaps (1.27). The one answer that subtracts the descent lies least far of all 60.
  - Where the answers leave the original text: 51 of 60 at the fourth token, the other 9 at step 15 or 18. At that token the edit moves one near-tie by 0.45 to 0.68 of a score in every answer (a2-path 0.24 to 0.45, where 40 left there). From there the held rows are rows of a text the model no longer writes. That the opening causes the damage is not shown.
  - The fit's holds are loose, and they do something. At each matrix alone the rows asked to stay moved about 0.43 times as far as the diverging rows in both runs. After each matrix B2's held correct rows are 0.77 to 0.97 (median 0.91) as far from their original state as its unheld ones, and the unheld ones end closer than a2-path's correct rows did (0.062 against 0.086 after the last matrix). The held rows still end 0.009 to 0.048 from where they were. For scale only, two ways of running the original model on the same rows differ by 0.0002 to 0.0048; that is a numerical reference, not a tolerance shown to keep a text (Astra's two corrections, November 10).

- **c2-path** (step 6, declared November 10): the C rung. It starts from a2-path's model and corrects its 18 matrices in path order, by the same fit, here pulling towards a2-path's matrix. The rows are round one's: the original text of an answer through the first token a2-path's own answer changes. For the 12 answers a2-path damaged (104 rows) they are asked for the original model's state; for the 17 it repaired (265 rows) for a2-path's own state; both are fixed references and every row counts the same. A reading of the inputs made first says the two asks meet on nearly the same inputs at the opening (0.0% to 0.8% of a damaged answer's input lies outside what the repaired answers' inputs span), so the fit is expected to compromise there; the model's own answers judge it. Membership, fixed references, the correction centred at a2-path and both halves in the fit are Astra's conditions. What it leaves unconstrained (the question's positions and everything after the first changed token) is in `runs/c2-path/declaration.json`.

  - Result in `runs/c2-path/result.json`. The fit served neither ask: it split by step, not by answer. On the given text the damaged answers' rows keep 0.38 to 0.54 of a2-path's action and the repaired answers' same opening steps 0.45 to 0.68, with a correction of 0.9 to 3.4% of a2-path's change of each matrix.
  - Its own answers: 3 of the 12 answers a2-path broke are complete, 10 of the 17 it repaired stay complete, 2 of the 13 it had not repaired, 13 of the 18 it kept; 12 of 30 wrong and 16 of 30 correct in all (a2-path 17 and 18); round one 5 of 21 and 2 of 7 (7 and 3). By the judge's field 27 of the 30 answers that subtracted the descent no longer do (a2-path 29).
  - The fourth-token preference did not move back at all: 0.26 to 0.48 of a score (median 0.35) against a2-path's 0.24 to 0.45 (0.34); 42 answers leave their text there (40).
  - Its own traces over places 142 to 313: the answers that were wrong lie 0.87 of the way to the correct pattern (a2-path 0.79) and 1.44 gaps to the side (1.04); the correct answers travelled 1.42 gaps (1.27).
  - A model within 3.4% of a2-path's change of each matrix is not a2-path: 20 of 88 answers changed completeness (15 lost, 5 gained), 16 of 60 texts are the same, and the trace reading moved by 0.08. Differences of that size between rungs are within what the texts alone produce. The C rung as round one built it is not available at layers 0 to 8 with these rows: the two sets of rows are the same inputs.

- **c2-protected** (step 6, declared November 10): a reading beside c2-path, not the C rung. The same build with the damaged answers alone in the fit: a2-path's action is cancelled on their 104 rows and nothing is asked on the repaired ones. It says what cancelling a2-path's action at the opening does when nothing competes with it. Astra's limit on its reading: if the repair is lost, that shows this correction lost it, not that the repair went with the opening.

  - Result in `runs/c2-protected/result.json`. On the given text the damaged answers' rows returned most of the way at all 18 matrices (0.01 to 0.05 of a2-path's action left along it; 0.08 to 0.22 of it in distance), the repaired answers' same steps went with them (0.07 to 0.14 left), and their later steps kept it (0.98 to 1.01). The correction is 2.1 to 9.1% of a2-path's change.
  - Its own answers: 3 of the 12 answers a2-path broke are complete, 7 of the 17 it repaired, 4 of the 13 it had not, 11 of the 18 it kept; 11 of 30 wrong and 14 of 30 correct in all (a2-path 17 and 18). 28 of 30 no longer subtract the descent on the judge's field.
  - The fourth-token preference moved back only part of the way: median 0.28 of a score against a2-path's 0.34; 31 answers leave their text there against 40. What carries the rest is not shown; the question's positions are a plausible route (Astra's correction: the rows did not return entirely, and a preference is not proportional to how far rows moved).
  - Its own traces over places 142 to 313: the answers that were wrong lie 0.95 of the way to the correct pattern (a2-path 0.79) and 1.16 gaps to the side (1.04); the correct answers travelled 1.24 gaps (1.27).
  - The reading along the gap does not go with complete answers: 0.95 with 11 complete here, c2-path 0.87 with 12, a2-path 0.79 with 17, b2-path 0.63 with 4. It goes with the calculation the edit aims at, repaired in 27 to 30 of 30 in all four.

- **d2-path** (step 6, declared November 10): the D rung. a2-path's construction built again from the original, the same rows, targets and 18 matrices, with one change: the size of each matrix's change is counted by what it does to the inputs of ordinary requests, not by its weights (the second moment of the matrix's inputs over round one's eight calibration requests, read through the model as it stands, plus a floor measured by how well seven of the requests predict the eighth; it replaces round one's chosen 5%). Tested as every rung on its own answers and traces, and away from the questions: this model and a2-path's answer round one's 12 ordinary requests and 12 controls on their own.

  - Result in `runs/d2-path/result.json`. All 18 matrices were written; each changes 6.3 to 14.5% of its matrix (a2-path 5.3 to 12.2%). On the answers' rows the build did what a2-path's did: the rows asked to move moved 0.86 to 1.01 times as far.
  - Its own traces over places 142 to 313: the answers that were wrong lie 1.05 of the way to the correct pattern (a2-path 0.79) with 0.78 gaps to the side (1.04), and are the only ones of the round to end nearer the original correct pattern than they began (0.88 of the distance; a2-path 1.15; over the whole path still 1.09). Answer by answer, 25 of the 30 end nearer it (a2-path 11 of 30; Astra). The correct answers travelled 0.73 gaps (1.27).
  - Its own answers: the available energy is right in 23 of the 30 wrong answers and 23 of the 30 correct ones (a2-path 17 and 18), and 28 of 30 no longer subtract the descent on the judge's field. 7 of each 30 are complete (17 and 18); round one 4 of 21 and 3 of 7. 31 of the 60 state the required energy as climb plus descent (a2-path 1, the original 4), 28 of them with the available energy right: the descent is counted on both sides. Every model of the round except a2-path does this more often than the original (5 to 11 of 60 in the six others).
  - Where the traces part (`ladder parting`, an exploratory reading after the result: not declared, not a test, not a rule for where to edit): up to place 263 the answers with the available energy right project on or past the correct pattern's coordinate whatever they do next. At places 312 to 335 those with the required energy not right project at -0.09 and those with both energy fields right at 0.87, and the coordinate of 16 originally correct answers went from 0.95 to -0.28 there. Both groups lie two gaps or more to the side there, so it is a difference of projection, not a return to the wrong pattern (Astra). The bin was chosen on this model and then orders the few such answers of b2-path and both C2 runs. Why is not shown.
  - The fourth-token preference is where the original left it (median -0.05 of a score against a2-path's 0.34), and 3 answers leave their text there against 40. 33 leave it at step 11, opening the heading 'Initial State (Bottom of descent)', which the original writes in 16 of the 60 answers and this model in 52.
  - Away from the questions: on four unseen ordinary requests the states after each matrix are 0.60 to 0.74 as far from the original as a2-path's (predicted: under half); on the eight requests the measure saw, 0.28 to 0.48. a2-path moves the states of ordinary text too (0.027 to 0.118 from the original after each matrix on the held-out requests). No ordinary request keeps the original's text under either model; the six language controls keep it under both.
  - Against its declaration: the measure entered, weakly on unseen text. The calculation aimed at, the traces and the fourth token came out as predicted or beyond. The count of complete answers, predicted only as more than b2-path's 4, is 7, and an error that was not declared is in half the answers.

- **a2-path-steps** (step 5, declared November 10): a2-path's edit read at three fractions of its size (0.268, 0.579, 0.786, computed from where each answer would first leave its original text), each model answering on its own. No new fit. Asked for by Marcos to understand the rate of change that broke the correct answers: a2-path's answers part from the original's at the fourth token, where the original's preference is a few tenths of a score and the edit moves it by about a third. It reports two curves, answers staying on their text and answers adding the descent; it does not choose a step.

  - Result in `runs/a2-path-steps/result.json`. Complete answers of the overlap, wrong then correct, at 0, 0.268, 0.579, 0.786 and 1 of the edit: 0 and 30, 7 and 15, 5 and 8, 8 and 9, 17 and 18. A fraction of a2-path is not a gentler a2-path: no fraction repairs more than it breaks, and the whole edit is far better than 0.786 of it.
  - The descent calculation follows the size (30, 13, 2, 2, 1 answers still subtract it). Whole-answer completeness does not, and which answers break or are repaired changes between neighbouring sizes (6 correct answers break at every size, 3 at none), so one model's count of complete answers is one draw.
  - Both of its construction predictions failed: the correct answers break at least as often at 0.268 as at the whole edit (15 against 12), and they break while keeping their original opening (6 of 12 at 0.268). A likely reason a fraction does worse, not tested: each matrix was fitted on what the earlier ones left at full size. A rate would have to sit inside the build.

- **controlled-sweep-1** (step 5, stopped November 10 at 6:51 PM CT; rejected precision): the proposed first sweep used a copy of the original model with the 70 matrices kept as float32, the same values. Marcos rejected that construction before any fit. The copy and partial records are preserved; the stop is in `runs/controlled-sweep-1/result.json`, and the following describes the unexecuted training proposal, not completed work. The matrices are changed in path order, each only as far as it brings the fitting answers' wrong pattern toward the correct one where the two diverge while every bound from the original answers' own variance holds; 15 and 15 answers are fitted, in three groups that take turns being solved on, held and only read, and 15 and 15 enter no fit. Every pass feeds an answer's own tokens one at a time. The finished model is read twice, in either order: the replay on the unused answers, and the model's own answers, which is the test and is read by the trace alone.

  - Declared before it ran: the replay is expected not to pass on every bound, because in the development map 23 of the 70 matrices, each alone, leave 1 to 14 of 960 direction cells over their bound on unused answers; the own answers' wrong pattern is expected to move toward the correct one, with less tube loss than the unedited copy's own answers and than the original file's second answers read. Whether every budget of the own answers holds is not predicted.
  - Three of its solver's work numbers were still being measured when it was declared (rows queued behind another project's lease on the card); they are read before the build starts, and the declaration says so.
  - Stopped at 6:51 PM CT before any fit; see its `result.json`.
  - Its float32 copy of the model (9.9 GB) was removed on November 11: it held the original's values and no weight update (`result.json`), so the run's own `start` makes it again from the original file. Every record of the run stays.

- **controlled-bf16-1** (step 5, declared November 10; ended November 11 at its third matrix, not accepted): the first sweep of the controlled training in the file's own BF16. The 70 matrices are changed in path order in a copy of the original file, each only as far as it brings the fitting answers' wrong pattern toward the correct one where the two diverge while every bound from the original answers' own variance holds; a step is written into BF16 as a whole, and which matrix is kept is decided among BF16 matrices that hold every bound on each third of the fitting answers and on all of them together. 15 and 15 answers are fitted, 15 and 15 enter no fit, and every pass feeds an answer's own tokens one at a time. Its first four commands read only the original file (baseline, reference, reproduced, own-start) and fix every bound, floor and budget before any matrix is written. The finished model is read twice, in either order: the replay on the answers no fit used, and its own answers by the trace alone, which is the test. Declaration, with its prediction and what would weaken it, in `runs/controlled-bf16-1/declaration.json`.

  - Declared before it ran: the original file's reading is expected to lie far inside the sampling bounds of its recorded rows, so the floors free nothing at layer 0; the build is expected to work at its bounds from the first matrix that fills one; the replay is expected not to pass on every bound, because single cells of the unused answers already leave theirs when one matrix is changed alone (one size cell at layer 3's attention, two direction cells at layer 7's); the own answers' wrong pattern is expected to move toward the correct one, and whether every budget holds is not predicted.
  - Running since November 10, 9:06 PM CT. The four commands that read the original file alone have ended: baseline 11:37 PM CT, reference 11:41 PM CT, reproduced 12:51 AM CT, own-start 1:05 AM CT (stopped by its own rule, below). The build started at 1:07 AM CT on November 11, wrote layer 0's attention (3.6e-6 of the matrix changed, every bound held, the fitting answers left 0.99976 of their tube loss) and stopped at 1:19 AM CT at layer 0's feed-forward before writing it. It went on from that matrix with the correction below.
  - The reference, against what was declared: no bound is raised by a floor at any of the 105 merges. Read twice one token at a time, the original file gives the same rows at every attention and feed-forward merge and rows at most 3e-7 apart at the layer merges, so the floors free nothing at layer 0, as declared. The merge rebuilt from a matrix's input and the state it adds to lies past a bound from the engine's own output of the original model at 11 direction cells in 6 merges (680 times at layer 0's attention, 48 at its feed-forward, 7.7 at layer 3's feed-forward) and at 1.000 to 1.002 of 10 size cells: cells where the original answers do not vary and the bound is next to nothing. That is the distance between the two forms a merge is read in, before any edit; it frees nothing and proves no edit wrong (Astra).
  - Revised November 11, before any matrix was written (the declaration's `revisions`): 80 iterations of a linear solve in place of 40, the search for the part of a step as fine as before until a part holds and to an eighth of the step after, the writing measure made to fit the card at the 20 matrices that are 12288 wide (the solver as declared ran out of memory there), and a step written on the card by a faster form of the same operations that gives the same weights bit for bit. The readings are in `records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/` (`bf16-choose.jsonl` lines 13 to 27) and `records/development/2026-11-10-speed-review/`. Not found: why the linear solve converges slowly where the bounds bind; seven other preconditioners were read and none is better at every ridge.
  - Reproduced, against what was declared: the original file read a second time holds every bound at all 105 merges against the frozen reference, and its tube loss left is 0.9999999 to 1.0000002 of the whole.
  - Revised November 11 after the build stopped (the declaration's `revisions`), two faults the run found by doing what it declares. The writing measure could not be factored at layer 0's feed-forward, and by the rule a failed factorization ends the work with nothing damped. The cause is the solver, not the measure: it kept the size bounds' part of the measure in float32, and at this matrix that part has entries up to 2.6e7, so its rounding (eigenvalues from -1.42 to 0.57) is larger than the stage's ridge once the ridge is halved seven times (0.40). Kept in double precision, as it is summed, the measure factors at all 42 places of that matrix's solve, against 32 of 42 in float32, and the matrix solves through on stored rows: 0.776 of the fitting answers' tube loss left, 0.806 for the answers no fit used (`bf16-choose.jsonl` line 28). No development reading had been taken at this matrix. The second fault is in the rule for a whole answer: it asked every answer to end by itself, and three of the 60 recordings (`expanded-001-h1`, `expanded-001-h3`, `expanded-044-h1`) ran into their limit of 768 tokens. The original file's second answers repeat all 60 recordings token for token, those three included, and own-start refused them. Astra corrected the rule before any edited model's own answer existed: an answer is whole as its recording was (ended by itself within the limit, or, where the recording ran into the limit, that or run into the same limit, with every decision kept); whether every answer ended by itself stays on record as the plain fact. The members, the limit and every allowance are as they were, and the floors are made from the captures already saved.
  - The build was started again at 1:39 AM CT with the corrected solver, from layer 0's feed-forward. Layer 0's attention stands as written; after a restart it can no longer be taken back, as the command is declared, and if a later stop is owed to that it is a limit of this resumed search and not a sign that no change would have held (Astra).
  - Own-start, run again on the saved captures (ended 1:51 AM CT; `own/start.json`, the stopped record beside it as `own/start.stopped-1.json`): 60 complete observations under the recordings' own protocol, three of them still cut at the limit as their recordings are, so not 60 answers that ended by themselves. The original file's second answers repeat all 60 recordings token for token. No floor lies above its sampling term in any of the 100,800 cells of any of the four quantities, on the answers no fit used, the fitting answers or all of them: the budgets of the own answers are their sampling terms, and with nothing changed the tube loss reads 1.00000001 of the whole.
  - **Result** (`runs/controlled-bf16-1/result.json`): the sweep ended at 2:53 AM CT on November 11 at its third matrix, layer 1's attention, not accepted. There is no finished model, so the replay, the own answers and the verdict were not run, and the hypothesis is not tested by this run. Layer 0's feed-forward, inside every bound of its own merge, left the fitting answers 0.727 of their tube loss there. Read through the engine, it left one direction cell of the layer's last merge at 1.35 of its bound, and 0.852 of the change was kept. Layer 1 attention's own merge then stood outside 19 cells (largest 23 times its bound); its own solve brought none back and no part of the feed-forward change did, down to 1/4096 of it.
  - What ended it, read after the sweep (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/`: `inspect-2-attention.json`, `inspect-2-attention-position0-only.json`, `bounds-spread.out`): 14 of the 19 cells are at places 0 to 3, 10, 17, 18 and 21, the opening steps of the answers, where the answers of a group hardly differ. Their bounds are 2e-5 to 5e-3 of the median bound of that merge; the smallest is 4.3e-10 (the wrong group's size at place 3) and the cell furthest past its bound, the wrong group's size at place 2, has 5.9e-10, against a median of 2.1e-5. The movements themselves are small: 1.4e-8 at that cell, and at most 1.1e-7 among the sizes of the opening cells, 0.0053 of the median size bound. With layer 0's feed-forward put back to the original in a scratch copy, so that only the first matrix's change of 3.6e-6 is left, the wrong group's size at place 2 is still at 1.38 of its bound at layer 1's attention: taking back the feed-forward alone, in any part, could not have ended inside. The build takes back one matrix and no further, restart or not, so this is where that search ends; it is not a showing that no edit in the file's format holds these cells (corrected November 11 from Astra's review, `records/retrospectives/2026-11-11-cell-allowance-review-astra.md`). The bounds of one merge span seven to eight orders of magnitude over its cells, the tightest always at places 0 to 3, and the floor that was to keep a bound from being less than what the model does not repeat is nothing there because the engine repeats itself. The other five cells are at places 149 to 151, where the wrong answers begin to diverge, 1.3 to 2.75 times past bounds near the median.
  - The original answers at those cells, read on stored rows after the sweep by Astra's formulas (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/allowance-reading.out`; a description, it changes no bound, target, floor or verdict): at places 0 to 3 every answer of a group has generated the same text before the decision, and the answers differ there by 1e-10 to 2e-7 in the size and 1e-9 to 5e-6 in the direction, against medians of 3e-4 to 8e-4 and 7e-3 to 4e-2 over a merge's places. With any one answer left out they stay small, between 0.21 and 1.04 of themselves, and none is exactly zero (1.04 is the most any 30 answers can read; at 0.21 one answer carries about four fifths of the scatter); this describes these 30 recordings, not a population's variance. In all 19 cells the rows moved less than the original answers differ from each other there (0.03 to 0.77 of that variance); what they are past is that variance over 30, the precision of the group's mean. Where both groups are outside at one place they moved by nearly the same amounts (the size at place 1: 4.96e-8 and 5.04e-8), which does not show one movement common to both: the signs and directions are not on record. The scalar moderation of the size variances, a naive model that takes a group's places for one population, fits a prior worth about one degree of freedom against 29: the places' log variances vary 44 to 90 times more than sampling alone would make them. Astra's reading of the comparison (the same review, commit 146905c2) adopts no floor from it, exempts no place and keeps the allowance as it is: the solver is what is repaired next. The edited model's rows were not kept, so its movement is not decomposed further.
  - The declaration names this outcome under the construction, not the hypothesis: the bounds as built do not carry, or leave no room in the file's format. Which bounds is now known: those of the cells where the original answers do not vary. How a cell's allowance is defined is step 5 of the method and Marcos's to change (his view of November 10: the training might be too sensitive to the small discrepancies at certain moments and need a way to normalize); what floor or normalization follows from the records, derived and not picked, is open with Astra.

More iterations are not the remedy: on stored rows layer 3's attention reads 0.4689 at 80 iterations and 0.4693 at 320. Astra's answer on a well-posed subproblem at a binding bound is in `records/retrospectives/2026-11-10-stable-training-review-astra.md`. On those rows every bound of layer 1's attention comes back inside in three to seven rounds (two to three minutes) with the matrix changed by 0.43 to 0.45%; the committed bringing back had left the furthest part 26 times its bound. Astra's reading (`records/retrospectives/2026-11-11-restoration-and-progress-astra.md`): the least-change bringing back stays, a matrix that was brought back may pull, and one at which no pull is kept stays as it was brought back. The cause is the engine's arithmetic, not the cells: where it reads several tokens at once, at the question's positions and at each answer's first step, it rounds what goes into a matrix to the file's BF16 before multiplying, and a solve had taken those rows as recorded. On the original file at all 70 matrices the merge rebuilt with the input as the engine multiplies it is the engine's own output to 1e-6, no part past 0.06 of a bound; rebuilt from the input as recorded it lies up to 680 bounds away, which is the distance the reference records between the two forms. The solver now takes a matrix's input as the engine multiplies it and counts everything from the engine's own original output. With it the same matrix is brought back inside in nine runs of nine, and the engine's reading of the written matrix is the solve's: every bound inside, the same tube loss to ten digits, the furthest part at 0.9864 of its bound against 0.9865. The build now takes a pull back toward the matrix as it was brought back, never toward one that stands outside its own bounds. The frozen patterns, bounds and floors are as they were; they are made from the rows as recorded, and whether the bounds at the first place and the size's expression floor there are remade in the engine's form is Astra's to say. No sweep has run with this yet.

At each answer's first step, which the engine decides at the prompt's last position while it reads the prompt at once, that input is rounded to the file's BF16; at every later step it is as recorded. Astra's decision of November 11 (`records/retrospectives/2026-11-11-native-reference-decision-astra.md`): the next sweep's reference is made fresh in this form, and its gap and chance have to be its target overlap's, so the overlap is recorded in the same form first. `state-overlap` stays as it is, with everything built on it.

The pull of the matrix before it was halved back twelve times and at none did the merge come back inside, so the sweep ended as declared. This is this configuration's result at layer 9's feed-forward; the run does not test the hypothesis, and the earlier repairs and their transfer stand as recorded.
  - What the sweep shows of the split. The engine's own output holds every bound at all 28 merges read after a written matrix (three a layer for layers 0 to 8, and layer 9's attention), the furthest part at 0.568 to 0.997 of its bound; no bound was drawn in. Ten of nineteen matrices pull, to 0.620 of the tube loss at layer 6's feed-forward, where under the first allowance every matrix past the first layer left 0.976 to 0.996; matrices three to eight leave 0.658, 0.799, 0.871, 0.956, 0.971 and 0.984, each less than the same matrix left then, the last two above the 0.97 the declaration named. Through layer 7 a bringing back changes 0.14% to 0.61% of a matrix against 2.1% to 6.8%. What the split leaves open is the means: every matrix after the first arrived with a group's mean past its limit, 2.1 to 30.8 times at its own merge, and from layer 8 bringing them back took 1.04%, 8.87%, 3.77% and an unwritten 4.11% of a matrix. Of the eighteen paths after a bringing back, fifteen ended with every branch's read third past a bound and three with no solve taking a step (layers 5, 7 and 8's feed-forward); nine matrices kept under 0.0001 of the tube loss beyond their bringing back. Six bringing backs had rounds of which no part was taken.
  - What follows, none of it a change of a bound. The solver's part is this session's: read on rows kept from the left model at layer 9's feed-forward whether the same bringing back ends inside on another run and which earlier changes put the size's means outside there; read what the three paths that took no step were refused for; finish the development reading of a pull that holds all three thirds' bounds in its step (`records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/README.md`). That pull changes a declared part of step 5 and is Marcos's to approve: Astra put the question to him on November 11 (`records/retrospectives/2026-11-11-all-fitting-constraints-method-check-astra.md`); no answer is on record. The limits on the means are Astra's to weigh. This project loads one card at a time until Marcos says otherwise. The two stops without a shutdown do not share two loaded cards: on November 10 at 8:28 PM CT card 1 was idle (2% load, 28 W) and card 0 was stepping between 44 W and 364 to 382 W; on November 11 at 7:36 PM CT both were loaded (hostshield's telemetry, `~/.local/state/hostshield/telemetry`; weight-research-perfomance's reading in `records/development/2026-11-10-speed-review/README.md`, "What the cards were doing at the two stops"). What they share is card 0's load stepping by hundreds of watts. No cause is shown.
  - Read after it ended, on the rows kept at layer 9's feed-forward (development, November 11 and 12; no bound, reference or model file changed; `records/development/2026-11-10-controlled-training/README.md`, the position 19 rows). The same bringing back, run again five times on card 0, ends inside every bound five times of five, in 12 to 20 rounds with 4.11% to 4.16% of the matrix changed: the sweep's end was one round at the smallest ridge of which no part left less outside, at which the solver ended the solve. Of 59 rounds played at that ridge from states kept late in those solves, one was such a round. Played eighteen times from the state kept there, the solve ends outside three times of four where it ends at that round and inside fourteen times of fourteen where the round is made again with the margins it has just grown; the solver makes it again from the commit that records this. What it does not reach is a state where the least step is too small for the file to hold; one was made only by beginning a solve again from the matrix it stood on, which is therefore not adopted. What the first step of that bringing back answers to is no one family of bounds (with a family, or both means, not held in the script's memory it stays 2.6% to 3.7% of the matrix) but the reading of each third beside all the answers together: with all together read alone it is 0.83% against 3.72%. That is one state and one round, the changed search's outcome and not a reading of whether the thirds' limits are needed; Astra keeps the approved readings and bounds as they are (`records/retrospectives/2026-11-11-thirds-pool-and-mean-estimators-astra.md`). Nor is that first step the least step at the ten passes a round has: 3.72% at pass 10, 2.89% at pass 80 and still falling; what to speed the splitting with is open and is in no sweep yet. Holding the wrong group's mean size to where the two groups' sizes differ is not what this bringing back costs (4.05% of the matrix with it, 4.11% to 4.16% without). The left model is in the bucket and its local copy is removed (`records/round-two-traces-release-2026-11-10.json`). Card 0's clock is locked at 2400 MHz since 11:28:48 PM CT on November 11, and at Marcos's word, relayed by weight-research-perfomance, two cards may be loaded again (commits 243827e9 and 9897345d).

## Not the method
Three runs of November 9 followed the removed plan: `frame-decisions`, `frame-gap` and `gap-patch-test`. They read single tokens, compared matched pairs and added the pair differences at one merge at a time. Marcos had them and their code deleted on November 9. Nothing is built on them. They remain in Git history up to commit `a011615a`.

## Kept inputs (original model only, read-only)
All paths are under `studies/04-round-2-overlap/inputs/`.
- `acquisition/`: question manifests and cases (historical, discovery, expanded, teacher); the original natural responses on both cards; cohort captures and archives; the qualification records.
- `acquisition/reserved-40-original/`: the original model on the reserved 40.
- `acquisition/reserved-40b/`: the frozen questions and the original model's sealed responses. Unread.
- `observation/original-model-traces/full-response-turns/`: the full-response direction and size traces at all 105 merges for the 45 eligible original responses (18 failures, 27 successes).
- `observation/original-model-traces/matrix-inputs/`: the original model's native inputs to each selected matrix for those 45.
- `observation/response-correspondence/`: passage roles and token alignment of the original answers.
- `observation/group-observation`, `individual-observation`, `execution-correspondence`, `context-index`: observations of the original model.

## Earlier work
- `records/retrospectives/2026-11-09-round-two-repairs/`: what the deleted edits repaired and what carried over.
- `records/retrospectives/2026-11-09-decision-margins/`: what the deleted edits moved, token by token. The removed plan took its vocabulary from here. A record, not a method.
- Runs **overlap**, **acquisition-single-card** and **originals-overlap**: overlays over whole passages of a 45-question cohort. They stopped at describing the group difference and built no edit. Their reports are `runs/overlap/OVERLAP.md`, `runs/acquisition-single-card/RESULT.md` and `runs/originals-overlap/GROUP-OVERLAYS.md`.

## The tree's cleanup — from November 15

A run line on this page may name a program that is no longer in the tree. From November 15 the cleanup Marcos gave the solver's seat and Performance removes every executable that takes tokens, words, answers or the values of the stream as a measure of progress, and what only those used; capture, raw extraction, arithmetic still used, and every declaration, result and run file stay as they were written. Each removed program is listed with the sha256 of its last bytes and the commit that holds them: `records/development/2026-11-15-tree-cleanup/retired-programs.json` (the solver's side; that folder's `README.md` holds the rule and the checks), `records/development/2026-11-12-path-forward/retired-state-value-readers.json` and `retired-progress-readers.json` (Performance's side), and the `retired-programs.json` of the runs that earlier retirements named. No run's status changed with it.

The old solver left `code/signal-trace/pipeline/weight_edit` on November 15 at 5:18 PM CT, in one step with Performance (wave 2 of that record). `tube.py`, `ladder.py`, `gap_run.py`, `gap_targets.py`, `whole_context_score.py`, `context_score.py`, `context_targets.py`, `a2_pattern.py` hold only what the capture and the native readings take, each kept definition the same bytes as before. `correction.py` and `field.py` are stubs, as `mixture.py` has been since wave 1: a parameter on this page that cites one of them finds what it rests on written at the same path, and the code at the commit the file names. Every frozen reader protocol (`controlled-bf16-1` to `-9`) binds `tube.py`, `ladder.py` and `gap_targets.py` as they were at its freeze, so no capture starts under one until Performance makes the protocol again. No run's status changed with it.

The pipeline packages no seat held left on November 15 at 6:01 PM CT (wave 3 of that record, 47fdeb8b): 73 files, among them the early overlap of the original cohort behind the runs `overlap` and `originals-overlap` (`pipeline/overlap`'s readings and their statistics), round one's observations (`pipeline/observation`) and the correspondence by the answers' text (`pipeline/correspondence`). Every reading among them compared the traces at places the answers' text lined up: two answers' steps paired by the same token, a numeral's position or a section's heading. `group_observation.py` and `complete_overlap.py` hold only what staying programs take, each kept definition the same bytes. The programs of the method's steps 1 to 3 as they ran (`trace_overlap.py`, `sent_overlap.py`, `input_overlap.py`, `complete_overlap.py`) stay, with capture, qualification against ordinary inference and the raw readers of recorded files. The 135 scripts inside run folders and the three earlier studies stay as written. No run's status changed with it.

What git's list did not show left on November 15 at 6:26 PM CT (wave 4 of that record, 0627e02f): 10 copies of the old solver that git did not track, `correction-a3ad3400.py` and `tube-a3ad3400.py`, pinned inside 5 folders of the runs `on-policy-33-original`, `overlap-chain-29-34-original`, `sign-33-only-original`, `sign-chain-29-34-original` as what earlier readings were made with. Each was the same bytes as commit a3ad3400 holds, so `git show a3ad3400:code/signal-trace/pipeline/weight_edit/tube.py` reads one again, and the records that name a copy by its path and sha256 are unchanged. The tracked programs were counted again in every language (589), and `codegraph.json` at the repository's root ranks `studies/` below live code in a search of the code. The reader protocol was made again at 6:18 PM CT as `controlled-bf16-10` (Performance, 54bf4cd6): what this section says above of `-1` to `-9` holds for those, and `-10` binds the cut files as they stand, so a cut of one of them means a new protocol. The audit of the one-pair chain by an Eli subagent, which Marcos asked for, is `records/retrospectives/2026-11-15-token-audit-eli.md` (c4882734, placed at 7:00 PM CT). Withdrawn from this paragraph: from 6:43 PM CT November 15 it said that the pairing of two answers' steps by their turn vectors (`complete_overlap.unit_steps` and `distance_path`) is not the computation's relation, and from 7:07 PM CT that this page's Vocabulary is not the definition of November 15. Both were a seat's reading written down as Marcos's. The Vocabulary's trace and overlap are his reports' own, and wherever this page says "by the computation alone" it means that overlap: answers laid on one another by their turns at all 105 merges, no token, number or word used. No run's status changed with it.

**The cleanup was reversed on November 15 at 7:55 PM CT.** Every program its four waves removed or cut is in the tree again with the bytes commit 69b7d13f holds (4:22 PM CT that day, before either cleanup seat's first removal): 264 files back whole, 19 rewritten files back to their whole forms, and the ten pinned copies inside the four runs' folders. Among them are `pipeline/weight_edit/ladder.py`, the solver of this page's step 5 as `a2-path` ran it, and `complete_overlap.py` whole, through which that solver takes its targets from the overlap. Marcos's word reached the seats through claude-research at 7:37 PM CT with his three reports; as the solver's seat understands him, the code that carries out his method was in the tree and the seats' replacements and removals broke it, so it comes back as it was and nothing more leaves until he has said which code stays. The rule this section's first paragraph gives for what leaves was claude-research's reading of his direction of 4:11 PM CT, as its message of 7:37 PM CT says. What the paragraphs above describe (the stubs, the cut modules, what left) is the tree from 4:42 to 7:55 PM CT and not the tree now. The reader protocol of form 4 binds six of the rewritten files as cut, so Performance makes it again. What left before 4:22 PM CT that day did not come back. The account, the counts and the checks: `records/development/2026-11-15-tree-cleanup/README.md`, "The programs are back". Nothing was run, and no run's status changed with it.

The [joint stock pre-choice run](runs/joint-stock-prechoice-training/result.json) ended cleanly after three installed updates when all downhill measurements disappeared. The fitted 95 J climb repaired, both descents remained wrong, and the 44 J control regressed. The 33-prompt paired evaluation remains unopened.
